```
         print(colorize(f"  Plan updated: items moved back to queue.", "dim"))
 
 
+_CLUSTER_INDIVIDUAL_THRESHOLD = 10
+
+
+def _check_cluster_guard(patterns: list[str], plan: dict, state: dict) -> bool:
+    """Return True if blocked by cluster guard, False if OK to proceed."""
+    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
+    findings = state.get("findings", {})
+    for pattern in patterns:
+        if pattern in clusters:
+            cluster = clusters[pattern]
+            # Filter to alive findings only — stale IDs should not count
+            ids = [
+                fid for fid in cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
+                if fid in findings and findings[fid].get("status") == "open"
+            ]
+            if len(ids) == 0:
+                print(colorize(
+                    f"\n  Cluster '{pattern}' is empty — add items before marking it done.\n",
+                    "yellow",
+                ))
+                print(colorize(
+                    f"  Use: desloppify plan cluster add {pattern} <finding-id>",
+                    "dim",
+                ))
+                return True  # blocked
+            if len(ids) <= _CLUSTER_INDIVIDUAL_THRESHOLD:
+                _print_cluster_guard(pattern, ids, state)
+                return True  # blocked
+    return False  # OK
+
+
+def _print_cluster_guard(cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str], state: dict) -> None:
+    findings = state.get("findings", {})
+    print(colorize(
+        f"\n  Cluster '{cluster_name}' has {len(finding_ids)} item(s) — mark them done individually first:\n",
+        "yellow",
+    ))
+    for fid in finding_ids:
+        f = findings.get(fid, {})
+        summary = f.get("summary", "(no summary)")[:80]
+        detector = f.get("detector", "?")
+        print(f"    {fid}  [{detector}]  {summary}")
+    print(colorize(
+        f"\n  Use: desloppify resolve <id> --status fixed --note '...' --attest '...'",
+        "dim",
+    ))
+    print(colorize(
+        f"  Or mark each done: desloppify plan done <id> --note '...' --confirm\n",
+        "dim",
+    ))
+
+
+def _is_synthetic_id(fid: str) -> bool:
+    """Return True if the ID is a synthetic workflow/triage item, not a real finding."""
+    return fid.startswith("triage::") or fid.startswith("workflow::") or fid.startswith("subjective::")
+
+
+def _resolve_synthetic_ids(patterns: list[str]) -> tuple[list[str], list[str]]:
+    """Separate synthetic IDs from real finding patterns.
+
+    Returns (synthetic_ids, remaining_patterns).
+    """
+    synthetic = [p for p in patterns if _is_synthetic_id(p)]
+    remaining = [p for p in patterns if not _is_synthetic_id(p)]
+    return synthetic, remaining
+
+
+def _blocked_triage_stages(plan: dict) -> dict[str, list[str]]:
+    """Return ``{stage_id: [blocked_by_ids]}`` for triage stages that can't run yet.
+
+    Uses the dependency graph and confirmed-stage metadata directly —
+    no state needed, no queue item construction.
+    """
+    from desloppify.app.commands.plan.triage_playbook import TRIAGE_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES
+    from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import TRIAGE_IDS, TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS
+
+    order_set = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
+    present = order_set & TRIAGE_IDS
+    if not present:
+        return {}
+
+    confirmed = set(plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {}).get("triage_stages", {}).keys())
+    stage_names = ("observe", "reflect", "organize", "commit")
+
+    blocked: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
+    for sid, name in zip(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS, stage_names):
+        if sid not in present or name in confirmed:
+            continue
+        deps = TRIAGE_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES.get(name, set())
+        unmet = sorted(
+            f"triage::{dep}" for dep in deps
+            if f"triage::{dep}" in present and dep not in confirmed
+        )
+        if unmet:
+            blocked[sid] = unmet
+    return blocked
+
+
```

```
1→"""Handler for ``plan triage`` subcommand.
     2→
     3→Implements a staged workflow: OBSERVE → REFLECT → ORGANIZE → COMMIT
     4→with a fast-track CONFIRM-EXISTING skip path.
     5→
     6→Stage gates validate **plan data enrichment**, not text reports:
     7→- OBSERVE: lightweight — write an analysis of themes/root causes (100 char min)
     8→- REFLECT: compare current findings against completed work (recurring patterns)
     9→- ORGANIZE: structural — each manual cluster must have description + action_steps
    10→- COMMIT: strategy must be substantive (200 char min) or "same"
    11→"""
    12→
    13→from __future__ import annotations
    14→
    15→import argparse
    16→from collections import defaultdict

    32→)

    36→from desloppify.engine._plan.epic_triage import (
    37→    build_triage_prompt,
    38→    collect_triage_input,
    39→    detect_recurring_patterns,
    40→    extract_finding_citations,
    41→)
    42→from desloppify.engine._plan.operations import append_log_entry
    43→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import review_finding_snapshot_hash
    44→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    45→from desloppify.engine.plan import (
    46→    TRIAGE_ID,

    48→    TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS,
    49→    load_plan,
    50→    purge_ids,
    51→    save_plan,
    52→)
    53→
    54→
    55→_STAGE_ORDER = ["observe", "reflect", "organize"]
    56→
    57→
    58→def _has_triage_in_queue(plan: dict) -> bool:
    59→    """Check if any triage stage ID (new or legacy) is in the queue."""
    60→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    61→    return bool(order & TRIAGE_IDS) or TRIAGE_ID in order
    62→
    63→
    64→def _inject_triage_stages(plan: dict) -> None:
    65→    """Inject all 4 triage stage IDs into the queue (fresh start)."""
    66→    order: list[str] = plan.setdefault("queue_order", [])
    67→    existing = set(order)
    68→    # Remove legacy ID if present
    69→    if TRIAGE_ID in existing:
    70→        order.remove(TRIAGE_ID)
    71→    for sid in TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS:
    72→        if sid not in existing:
    73→            order.insert(0 if sid == TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS[0] else len(order), sid)
    74→    # Re-insert in correct order at front
    75→    for sid in reversed(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS):
    76→        if sid in order:
    77→            order.remove(sid)
    78→    insert_at = 0
    79→    for sid in TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS:
    80→        order.insert(insert_at, sid)
    81→        insert_at += 1
    82→
    83→
    84→def _purge_triage_stage(plan: dict, stage_name: str) -> None:
    85→    """Purge a single triage stage ID from the queue."""
    86→    sid = f"triage::{stage_name}"
    87→    purge_ids(plan, [sid])
    88→
    89→
    90→def _cascade_clear_later_confirmations(stages: dict, from_stage: str) -> list[str]:
    91→    """Clear confirmed_at/confirmed_text on stages AFTER *from_stage*. Returns cleared names."""
    92→    try:
    93→        idx = _STAGE_ORDER.index(from_stage)
    94→    except ValueError:
    95→        return []
    96→    cleared: list[str] = []
    97→    for later in _STAGE_ORDER[idx + 1:]:
    98→        if later in stages and stages[later].get("confirmed_at"):
    99→            stages[later].pop("confirmed_at", None)
   100→            stages[later].pop("confirmed_text", None)
```

```
+def _run_batch_task(

+def _build_batch_tasks(
+    *,

+    log_files: dict[int, Path],

+    tasks: dict[int, object] = {}

+            prompt_path=prompt_files[batch_index],
+            output_path=output_files[batch_index],

+            run_codex_batch_fn=run_codex_batch_fn,
```

```
 from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
 
 _SCORECARD_SUBJECTIVE_AT_TARGET = bind_scorecard_subjective_at_target(
@@ -129,6 +135,106 @@ def _clear_provisional_override_flags(
     return cleared
 
 
+
+def _print_review_import_sync(state: dict, result: ReviewImportSyncResult) -> None:
+    """Print summary of plan changes after review import sync."""
+    new_ids = result.new_ids
+    print(colorize(
+        f"\n  Plan updated: {len(new_ids)} new review finding(s) added to queue.",
+        "bold",
+    ))
+    findings = state.get("findings", {})
+    for fid in sorted(new_ids)[:10]:
+        f = findings.get(fid, {})
+        print(f"    * [{short_finding_id(fid)}] {f.get('summary', '')}")
+    if len(new_ids) > 10:
+        print(colorize(f"    ... and {len(new_ids) - 10} more", "dim"))
+    print()
+    print(colorize("  New items added to end of queue.", "dim"))
+    print()
+    print(colorize("  View queue:            desloppify plan queue", "dim"))
+    print(colorize("  View newest first:     desloppify plan queue --sort recent", "dim"))
+    print()
+    print(colorize("  NEXT STEP:  desloppify plan triage", "yellow"))
+    print(colorize(
+        "  (Review new findings and decide whether to re-plan or accept current queue.)",
+        "dim",
+    ))
+
+
+def _sync_plan_after_review_change(state: dict, diff: dict) -> None:
+    """Best-effort: sync living plan after review import introduces new findings.
+
+    Also auto-resolves subjective dimension queue items that were covered
+    by this import and injects ``workflow::create-plan`` when all initial
+    reviews are complete.
+    """
+    try:
+        from desloppify.engine.plan import (
+            append_log_entry,
+            has_living_plan,
+            load_plan,
+            purge_ids,
+            save_plan,
+            sync_create_plan_needed,
+            sync_plan_after_review_import,
+        )
+
+        if not has_living_plan():
+            return
+
+        plan = load_plan()
+        result = sync_plan_after_review_import(plan, state)
+
+        # Auto-resolve subjective dimension items that are no longer unscored
+        from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import _current_unscored_ids
+        still_unscored = _current_unscored_ids(state)
+        order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
+        subjective_in_queue = [
+            fid for fid in order if fid.startswith("subjective::")
+        ]
+        covered_ids = [
+            fid for fid in subjective_in_queue
+            if fid not in still_unscored
+        ]
+        if covered_ids:
+            purge_ids(plan, covered_ids)
+
+        # Check if create-plan item should be injected
+        create_plan_result = sync_create_plan_needed(plan, state)
+        if create_plan_result.injected:
+            print(colorize(
+                "  Plan: reviews complete — `workflow::create-plan` queued. Run `desloppify plan`.",
+                "cyan",
+            ))
+
+        if result is not None:
+            append_log_entry(
+                plan,
+                "review_import_sync",
+                actor="system",
+                detail={
+                    "trigger": "review_import",
+                    "new_ids": sorted(result.new_ids),
+                    "added_to_queue": result.added_to_queue,
+                    "diff_new": diff.get("new", 0),
+                    "diff_reopened": diff.get("reopened", 0),
+                    "covered_subjective": covered_ids,
+                },
+            )
+
+        save_plan(plan)
+        if result is not None:
+            _print_review_import_sync(state, result)
+    except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
+        print(
+            colorize(
+                f"  Note: skipped plan sync after review import ({exc}).",
+                "dim",
+            )
+        )
+
+
 def do_import(
     import_file,
     state,
@@ -267,6 +373,10 @@ def do_import(
     state.update(working_state)
     state_mod.save_state(state, state_file)

+    if int(diff.get("new", 0) or 0) > 0 or int(diff.get("reopened", 0) or 0) > 0:
+        _sync_plan_after_review_change(state, diff)
+
     lang_name = lang.name
     narrative = narrative_mod.compute_narrative(
         state, NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="review")
```

```
diff --git a/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/queue_progress.py b/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/queue_progress.py
index 7d31e97..df968df 100644
--- a/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/queue_progress.py
+++ b/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/queue_progress.py
@@ -2,10 +2,190 @@
 
 from __future__ import annotations
 
+from dataclasses import dataclass
+
 from desloppify.core.exception_sets import PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS
 from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
 
 
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+# QueueBreakdown — single source of truth for queue numbers
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class QueueBreakdown:
+    """All numbers needed to render the standardized queue display."""
+
+    queue_total: int = 0
+    plan_ordered: int = 0
+    skipped: int = 0
+    subjective: int = 0
+    workflow: int = 0
+    focus_cluster: str | None = None
+    focus_cluster_count: int = 0
+    focus_cluster_total: int = 0
+
+
+def plan_aware_queue_breakdown(
+    state: dict,
+    plan: dict | None = None,
+) -> QueueBreakdown:
+    """Build a full :class:`QueueBreakdown` from a single ``build_work_queue`` call."""
+    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import QueueBuildOptions, build_work_queue
+
+    result = build_work_queue(
+        state,
+        options=QueueBuildOptions(
+            status="open",
+            count=None,
+            plan=plan,
+            collapse_clusters=True,
+            include_skipped=False,
+        ),
+    )
+
+    queue_total = result["total"]
+
+    # Count subjective and workflow items in the queue
+    items = result.get("items", [])
+    subjective = sum(
+        1 for item in items
+        if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension"
+    )
+    workflow = sum(
+        1 for item in items
+        if item.get("kind") in ("workflow_stage", "workflow_action")
+    )
+
+    # Plan-derived counts
+    plan_ordered = 0
+    skipped = 0
+    if plan:
+        skipped = len(plan.get("skipped", {}))
+        # plan_ordered = items that are in queue_order minus skipped
+        queue_order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
+        skipped_ids = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
+        plan_ordered = sum(1 for fid in queue_order if fid not in skipped_ids)
+
+    # Focus cluster info
+    focus_cluster = None
+    focus_cluster_count = 0
+    focus_cluster_total = 0
+    if plan:
+        active = plan.get("active_cluster")
+        if active:
+            focus_cluster = active
+            cluster_data = plan.get("clusters", {}).get(active, {})
+            focus_cluster_total = len(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
+            # Count how many cluster members are still in the queue
+            cluster_member_ids = set(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
+            open_findings = {
+                fid
+                for fid, f in state.get("findings", {}).items()
+                if f.get("status") == "open"
+            }
+            focus_cluster_count = len(cluster_member_ids & open_findings)
+
+    return QueueBreakdown(
+        queue_total=queue_total,
+        plan_ordered=plan_ordered,
+        skipped=skipped,
+        subjective=subjective,
+        workflow=workflow,
+        focus_cluster=focus_cluster,
+        focus_cluster_count=focus_cluster_count,
+        focus_cluster_total=focus_cluster_total,
+    )
+
+
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+# Formatting helpers — single source of truth for queue display
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+def format_queue_headline(breakdown: QueueBreakdown) -> str:
+    """The one-line Queue summary. Same format everywhere.
+
+    Examples::
+
+        Queue: 1934 items (292 planned · 23 skipped)
+        Queue: 1934 items
+    """
+    n = breakdown.queue_total
+    label = f"Queue: {n} item{'s' if n != 1 else ''}"
+
+    # Parenthesized segments
+    segments: list[str] = []
+    if breakdown.workflow > 0:
+        segments.append(f"{breakdown.workflow} planning step{'s' if breakdown.workflow != 1 else ''}")
+    if breakdown.plan_ordered > 0:
+        segments.append(f"{breakdown.plan_ordered} planned")
+    if breakdown.skipped > 0:
+        segments.append(f"{breakdown.skipped} skipped")
+    if breakdown.subjective > 0:
+        segments.append(f"{breakdown.subjective} subjective")
+    if segments:
+        sep = " \u00b7 "
+        detail = sep.join(segments)
+        return f"{label} ({detail})"
+    return label
+
+
+def format_queue_block(
+    breakdown: QueueBreakdown,
+    *,
+    frozen_score: float | None = None,
+) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
+    """Full queue block: focus banner + queue line + contextual hints.
+
+    Returns a list of ``(text, style)`` pairs ready for ``colorize()``.
+    """
+    lines: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
+
+    # Focus banner (prominent, separate)
+    if breakdown.focus_cluster:
+        focus_line = (
+            f"  Focus: `{breakdown.focus_cluster}` "
+            f"\u2014 {breakdown.focus_cluster_count}/{breakdown.focus_cluster_total}"
+            f" items remaining"
+        )
+        lines.append((focus_line, "cyan"))
+
+    # Frozen score line
+    if frozen_score is not None:
+        lines.append((
+            f"  Score (frozen at plan start): strict {frozen_score:.1f}/100",
+            "cyan",
+        ))
+
+    # Queue headline — always the same
+    lines.append((f"  {format_queue_headline(breakdown)}", "bold"))
+
+    # Contextual hints (dim)
+    if breakdown.focus_cluster:
+        lines.append((
+            f"  Unfocus: `desloppify plan focus --clear`"
+            f" \u00b7 Cluster details: `desloppify next --cluster {breakdown.focus_cluster} --count 10`",
+            "dim",
+        ))
+    elif breakdown.plan_ordered > 0 or breakdown.skipped > 0:
+        lines.append((
+            "  Details: `desloppify plan queue`"
+            " \u00b7 Skip: `desloppify plan skip <id>`",
+            "dim",
+        ))
+    else:
+        lines.append((
+            "  Start planning: `desloppify plan`",
+            "dim",
+        ))
+
+    return lines
+
+
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+# Legacy / backward-compatible helpers
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
 def plan_aware_queue_count(state: dict, plan: dict | None = None) -> int:
     """Count remaining plan-aware queue items (skips excluded, clusters collapsed)."""
     from desloppify.engine.work_queue import QueueBuildOptions, build_work_queue
```

```
+
+def _state_file_for_runtime(runtime) -> Path:
+    """Resolve the effective state file path for a command runtime."""
+    state_file = runtime.state_path
+    if isinstance(state_file, Path):
+        return state_file
+    return state_mod.STATE_FILE
+
+
+def _prune_excluded_findings(state: dict, pattern: str) -> list[str]:
+    """Drop findings whose file path matches a new exclusion pattern."""
+    findings = state.get("findings")
+    if not isinstance(findings, dict):
+        return []
+
+    removed_ids = [
+        finding_id
+        for finding_id, finding in findings.items()
+        if isinstance(finding, dict)
+        and matches_exclusion(str(finding.get("file", "")), pattern)
+    ]
+    for finding_id in removed_ids:
+        findings.pop(finding_id, None)
+    return removed_ids
+
+
+def _purge_removed_ids_from_plan(state_file: Path, removed_ids: list[str]) -> int:
+    """Remove pruned finding IDs from queue/skips/clusters in plan.json."""
+    if not removed_ids:
+        return 0
+    plan_file = plan_path_for_state(state_file)
+    plan = load_plan(plan_file)
+    purged = purge_ids(plan, removed_ids)
+    if purged:
+        save_plan(plan, plan_file)
+    return purged
 
 
 def cmd_exclude(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
```

```
     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.core import config as config_mod
     9→from desloppify.core.fallbacks import print_error
    10→
    11→
    12→def _save_state_or_exit(state: dict, state_file: str) -> None:
    13→    """Persist state with a consistent CLI error boundary."""
    14→    try:
    15→        state_mod.save_state(state, state_file)
    16→    except OSError as exc:
    17→        print_error(f"could not save state: {exc}")
    18→        sys.exit(1)
    19→
    20→
    21→def _save_config_or_exit(config: dict) -> None:
    22→    """Persist config with a consistent CLI error boundary."""
    23→    try:
    24→        config_mod.save_config(config)
    25→    except OSError as exc:
    26→        print_error(f"could not save config: {exc}")
    27→        sys.exit(1)
    28→
    29→
    30→__all__ = ["_save_config_or_exit", "_save_state_or_exit"]
```

```
1→"""Shared display utilities for command output."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→
     6→def short_finding_id(fid: str) -> str:
     7→    """Extract the 8-char hash suffix from a finding ID for compact display.
     8→
     9→    Finding IDs look like ``review::.::holistic::dim::identifier::abcdef12``.
    10→    Commands accept the hash suffix as a shorthand for the full ID.
    11→    """
    12→    if "::" in fid:
    13→        suffix = fid.rsplit("::", 1)[-1]
    14→        if len(suffix) >= 8:
    15→            return suffix[:8]
    16→    return fid
    17→
    18→
    19→__all__ = ["short_finding_id"]
    20→
```

```
1→"""Merge and dedupe logic for holistic review batch outputs."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Any
     6→
     7→from .batch_core import (
     8→    _accumulate_batch_scores,
     9→    _accumulate_batch_quality,
    10→    _compute_abstraction_components,
    11→    _compute_merged_assessments,
    12→    _finding_identity_key,
    13→    _finding_pressure_by_dimension,
    14→    assessment_weight,
    15→)
    16→from desloppify.intelligence.review.feedback_contract import (
    17→    REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY,
    18→)
    19→from desloppify.intelligence.review.finding_merge import (
    20→    merge_list_fields,
    21→    normalize_word_set,
    22→    pick_longer_text,
    23→    track_merged_from,
    24→)
    25→
    26→
    27→def _merge_finding_payload(existing: dict[str, Any], incoming: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    28→    merge_list_fields(existing, incoming, ("related_files", "evidence"))
    29→    pick_longer_text(existing, incoming, "summary")
    30→    pick_longer_text(existing, incoming, "suggestion")
    31→    track_merged_from(existing, str(incoming.get("identifier", "")).strip())
    32→
    33→
    34→def _should_merge_findings(existing: dict[str, Any], incoming: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
    35→    existing_summary = normalize_word_set(str(existing.get("summary", "")))
    36→    incoming_summary = normalize_word_set(str(incoming.get("summary", "")))
    37→    summary_similarity_signal = False
    38→    if existing_summary and incoming_summary:
    39→        overlap = len(existing_summary & incoming_summary)
    40→        union = len(existing_summary | incoming_summary)
    41→        summary_similarity_signal = bool(union and overlap / union >= 0.45)
    42→
    43→    existing_files = set(existing.get("related_files", []))
    44→    incoming_files = set(incoming.get("related_files", []))
    45→    file_overlap_signal = bool(existing_files and incoming_files and (existing_files & incoming_files))
    46→
    47→    existing_identifier = str(existing.get("identifier", "")).strip()
    48→    incoming_identifier = str(incoming.get("identifier", "")).strip()
    49→    identifier_signal = bool(
    50→        existing_identifier and incoming_identifier and existing_identifier == incoming_identifier
    51→    )
    52→
    53→    corroborating_signals = (
    54→        int(summary_similarity_signal)
    55→        + int(file_overlap_signal)
    56→        + int(identifier_signal)
    57→    )
    58→    if identifier_signal and (summary_similarity_signal or file_overlap_signal):
    59→        return True
    60→    return corroborating_signals >= 2
    61→
    62→
    63→def _append_batch_findings(
    64→    result: dict[str, Any],
    65→    findings: list[dict[str, Any]],
    66→) -> None:
    67→    for finding in result.get("findings", []):
    68→        if isinstance(finding, dict):
    69→            findings.append(finding)
    70→
    71→
    72→def _merge_finding_group(group: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    73→    """Merge one dedupe-key group using transitive connected components."""
    74→    if len(group) <= 1:
    75→        return list(group)
    76→
    77→    visited: set[int] = set()
    78→    components: list[list[int]] = []
    79→
    80→    for start in range(len(group)):
    81→        if start in visited:
    82→            continue
    83→        stack = [start]
    84→        component: list[int] = []
    85→        visited.add(start)
    86→        while stack:
    87→            node = stack.pop()
    88→            component.append(node)
    89→            source = group[node]
    90→            for probe in range(len(group)):
    91→                if probe in visited:
    92→                    continue
    93→                target = group[probe]
    94→                if _should_merge_findings(source, target) or _should_merge_findings(
    95→                    target, source
    96→                ):
    97→                    visited.add(probe)
    98→                    stack.append(probe)
    99→        components.append(sorted(component))
   100→
   101→    merged_components: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   102→    for indexes in sorted(components, key=lambda ids: ids[0]):
   103→        base = group[indexes[0]]
   104→        for idx in indexes[1:]:
   105→            _merge_finding_payload(base, group[idx])
   106→        merged_components.append(base)
   107→    return merged_components
   108→
   109→
   110→def _merge_findings_transitively(
   111→    findings: list[dict[str, Any]],
   112→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   113→    grouped: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]] = {}
   114→    for finding in findings:
   115→        grouped.setdefault(_finding_identity_key(finding), []).append(finding)
   116→
   117→    merged: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   118→    for group in grouped.values():
   119→        merged.extend(_merge_finding_group(group))
   120→    return merged
   121→
   122→
   123→def merge_batch_results(
   124→    batch_results: list[dict[str, Any]],
   125→    *,
   126→    abstraction_sub_axes: tuple[str, ...],
   127→    abstraction_component_names: dict[str, str],
   128→) -> dict[str, object]:
   129→    """Deterministically merge assessments/findings across batch outputs."""
   130→    score_buckets: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]] = {}
   131→    score_raw_by_dim: dict[str, list[float]] = {}
   132→    all_findings: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   133→    merged_dimension_notes: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
   134→    coverage_values: list[float] = []
   135→    evidence_density_values: list[float] = []
   136→    high_score_missing_issue_note_total = 0.0
   137→    abstraction_axis_scores: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]] = {
   138→        axis: [] for axis in abstraction_sub_axes
   139→    }
   140→
   141→    for result in batch_results:
   142→        _accumulate_batch_scores(
   143→            result,
   144→            score_buckets=score_buckets,
   145→            score_raw_by_dim=score_raw_by_dim,
   146→            merged_dimension_notes=merged_dimension_notes,
   147→            abstraction_axis_scores=abstraction_axis_scores,
   148→            abstraction_sub_axes=abstraction_sub_axes,
   149→        )
   150→        _append_batch_findings(result, all_findings)
   151→        high_score_missing_issue_note_total += _accumulate_batch_quality(
   152→            result,
   153→            coverage_values=coverage_values,
   154→            evidence_density_values=evidence_density_values,
   155→        )
   156→
   157→    merged_findings = _merge_findings_transitively(all_findings)
   158→    finding_pressure_by_dim, finding_count_by_dim = _finding_pressure_by_dimension(
   159→        merged_findings,
   160→        dimension_notes=merged_dimension_notes,
   161→    )
   162→
   163→    merged_assessments = _compute_merged_assessments(
   164→        score_buckets, score_raw_by_dim, finding_pressure_by_dim, finding_count_by_dim
   165→    )
   166→
   167→    merged_assessment_payload: dict[str, float | dict[str, object]] = {
   168→        key: value for key, value in merged_assessments.items()
   169→    }
   170→    component_scores = _compute_abstraction_components(
   171→        merged_assessments,
   172→        abstraction_axis_scores,
   173→        abstraction_sub_axes=abstraction_sub_axes,
   174→        abstraction_component_names=abstraction_component_names,
   175→    )
   176→    if component_scores is not None:
   177→        merged_assessment_payload["abstraction_fitness"] = {
   178→            "score": merged_assessments["abstraction_fitness"],
   179→            "components": list(component_scores),
   180→            "component_scores": component_scores,
   181→        }
   182→
   183→    return {
   184→        "assessments": merged_assessment_payload,
   185→        "dimension_notes": merged_dimension_notes,
   186→        "findings": merged_findings,
   187→        "review_quality": {
   188→            "batch_count": len(batch_results),
   189→            "dimension_coverage": round(
   190→                sum(coverage_values) / max(len(coverage_values), 1),
   191→                3,
   192→            ),
   193→            "evidence_density": round(
   194→                sum(evidence_density_values) / max(len(evidence_density_values), 1),
   195→                3,
   196→            ),
   197→            REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY: int(
   198→                high_score_missing_issue_note_total
   199→            ),
   200→            "finding_pressure": round(sum(finding_pressure_by_dim.values()), 3),
   201→            "dimensions_with_findings": len(finding_count_by_dim),
   202→        },
   203→    }
   204→
   205→
   206→__all__ = ["assessment_weight", "merge_batch_results"]
   207→
```

> AGENT

Important finding: the `batch_merge.py` uses `_append_batch_findings` which just appends findings to a flat list, then does `_merge_findings_transitively`. The old code used `_accumulate_batch_findings` which did dedup into a dict keyed by `_finding_identity_key`. The new approach collects all findings flat, then does a graph-based transitive merge at the end. This is a semantic change, not just an extraction. Let me verify the status_parts new files.

```
    14→_ACTION_TYPE_LABELS = {
    15→    "auto_fix": "Auto-fixable batch",
    16→    "reorganize": "Reorganize batch",
    17→    "refactor": "Refactor batch",
    18→    "manual_fix": "Grouped task",

    22→def scorecard_subjective(
    23→    state: dict,
    24→    dim_scores: dict,
    25→) -> list[dict]:
    26→    """Return scorecard-aligned subjective entries for current dimension scores."""
    27→    if not dim_scores:
    28→        return []
    29→    return scorecard_subjective_entries(
    30→        state,
    31→        dim_scores=dim_scores,
    32→    )
    33→
    34→
    35→def subjective_coverage_breakdown(
    36→    findings_scoped: dict,
    37→) -> tuple[int, dict[str, int], dict[str, int]]:
    38→    """Return open subjective-review count plus reason and holistic-reason breakdowns."""
    39→    return subjective_review_open_breakdown(findings_scoped)
    40→
    41→
    42→def is_auto_fix_command(command: str | None) -> bool:
    43→    cmd = (command or "").strip()
    44→    return cmd.startswith("desloppify fix ") and "--dry-run" in cmd
    45→
    46→
    47→def effort_tag(item: dict) -> str:
    48→    """Return a short effort/type tag for a queue item."""
    49→    if item.get("detector") == "review":
    50→        return "[review]"
    51→    if is_auto_fix_command(item.get("primary_command")):
    52→        return "[auto]"
    53→    return ""
    54→
    55→
    56→def render_grouped(items: list[dict], group: str) -> None:
    57→    grouped = group_queue_items(items, group)
    58→    for key, grouped_items in grouped.items():
    59→        print(colorize(f"\n  {key} ({len(grouped_items)})", "cyan"))
    60→        for item in grouped_items:
    61→            confidence = item.get("confidence", "medium")
    62→            tag = effort_tag(item)
    63→            tag_str = f" {tag}" if tag else ""
    64→            print(
    65→                f"    [{confidence}]{tag_str} {item.get('summary', '')}"
    66→            )
    67→
    68→
    69→def render_cluster_item(item: dict) -> None:
    70→    """Render an auto-cluster task card."""
    71→    member_count = int(item.get("member_count", 0))
    72→    action_type = item.get("action_type", "manual_fix")
    73→    cluster_name = item.get("id", "")
    74→    is_optional = bool(item.get("cluster_optional"))
    75→    if cluster_name == "auto/initial-review":
    76→        type_label = "Initial subjective review"
    77→    elif cluster_name == "auto/stale-review":
    78→        type_label = "Stale subjective review"
    79→    elif cluster_name == "auto/under-target-review":
    80→        type_label = "Optional re-review"
    81→    else:
    82→        type_label = _ACTION_TYPE_LABELS.get(action_type, "Grouped task")
    83→    optional_tag = " — optional" if is_optional else ""
    84→    print(colorize(f"  ({type_label}, {member_count} findings{optional_tag})", "bold"))
    85→    print(colorize("  " + "─" * 60, "dim"))
    86→    print(f"  {colorize(item.get('summary', ''), 'yellow')}")
    87→
    88→    members = item.get("members", [])
    89→    if members:
    90→        file_counts = Counter(m.get("file", "?") for m in members)
    91→        if len(file_counts) <= 5:
    92→            print(colorize("\n  Files:", "dim"))
    93→            for filename, count in file_counts.most_common():
    94→                print(f"    {filename} ({count})")
    95→        else:
    96→            print(colorize(f"\n  Spread across {len(file_counts)} files:", "dim"))
    97→            for filename, count in file_counts.most_common(3):
    98→                print(f"    {filename} ({count})")
    99→            remaining = len(file_counts) - 3
   100→            print(colorize(f"    ... and {remaining} more files", "dim"))
   101→

   106→            print(colorize(f"    ... and {len(members) - 3} more", "dim"))
   107→
   108→    cluster_name = item.get("id", "")
```

## Concrete Assessment of Uncommitted Changes in `app/commands/`

This diff set represents a large refactoring with three interleaved concerns: (1) the "synthesis" to "triage" rename, (2) extraction of large modules into smaller focused files, and (3) genuine feature additions (audit logging, cluster merge, triage guardrails, queue breakdown display, note length validation). I'll assess each area concretely.

---

### 1. plan/ -- Generally Well Done, Some Complexity Concerns

**Good changes:**

- **Audit logging (`append_log_entry` calls)** throughout `cluster_handlers.py`, `move_handlers.py`, `override_handlers.py` is a clean, consistent pattern. Every user-mutating action gets logged. The call sites are placed right before `save_plan()`, which is the correct position.

- **`_resolve_target()` in `move_handlers.py`** (lines 12-28) is a well-conceived helper that lets users use cluster names as `--target` arguments for `before`/`after` positioning. It resolves cluster names to first/last member IDs based on their position in queue_order. This is genuinely useful and well-implemented.

- **`_cmd_cluster_merge()` in `cluster_handlers.py`** (lines ~290-320) is a clean feature addition that delegates to `merge_clusters()` in the engine layer.

- **`_print_pattern_hints()` in `cluster_handlers.py`** (lines 25-33) -- useful UX improvement when no matches are found.

- **Overlap warning** in `_cmd_cluster_add()` (lines 77-90) is a thoughtful guard against accidental duplicate clustering.

**Concerns:**

- **`_resolve_target` is exported as a private function** via `__all__ = ["cmd_plan_move", "_resolve_target"]`. It's imported in `cluster_handlers.py` by its underscore-prefixed name. The function should be renamed to `resolve_target` if it's part of the cross-module API. This is a naming contract violation.

- **`cmd_plan_done()` in `override_handlers.py`** has grown significantly (lines 441-537). It now handles synthetic IDs, triage dependency chains, note length validation, cluster completion guards, and audit logging before finally delegating to `cmd_resolve`. This function does too many things. The cluster guard, triage dependency check, and synthetic ID resolution should be separate pre-flight checks rather than inline logic.

- **`_check_cluster_guard()` and `_blocked_triage_stages()`** in `override_handlers.py` are new helpers that are tightly coupled to `cmd_plan_done()` but are defined at module scope. They make sense as extracted functions, but `_blocked_triage_stages()` imports from `triage_playbook.py` and `stale_dimensions.py` at the function level -- indicating the module is accumulating knowledge about triage internals that belongs elsewhere.

**The synthesis-to-triage rename:**

- `triage_playbook.py` is a direct rename of `synthesis_playbook.py` -- all `SYNTH_CMD_*` constants became `TRIAGE_CMD_*`, `SYNTHESIS_STAGE_*` became `TRIAGE_STAGE_*`. The rename is mechanical and complete.

- `triage_handlers.py` (1715 lines) is significantly larger than the original `synthesize_handlers.py` (1028 lines). Beyond the rename, it:
  - Imports from a new `triage/` subdirectory (`triage.shared`, `triage.stage_helpers`, etc.)
  - Adds `_inject_triage_stages()` and `_purge_triage_stage()` for per-stage queue ID management
  - Adds `_cascade_clear_later_confirmations()` for invalidation logic
  - Replaces the single `SYNTHESIS_ID` with multiple `TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS`

  The split of the single "synthesis::pending" marker into four separate `triage::observe`, `triage::reflect`, `triage::organize`, `triage::commit` stage IDs is a genuine structural improvement -- it enables per-stage dependency tracking and individual resolution. However, at 1715 lines, `triage_handlers.py` should itself be further decomposed. The new `triage/` subdirectory (`shared.py`, `stage_helpers.py`, `stage_persistence.py`, `progress_render.py`, `reflect.py`, `reflect_dashboard.py`, `organize.py`) already extracts some logic, but the main handler is still very large.

---

### 2. review/ -- Over-Engineered Compatibility Wrappers, Good Extraction of batch_merge

**Bad pattern -- compatibility wrappers in batches.py:**

This is the weakest part of the diff. In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py`, four functions have been renamed with an `_impl` suffix, and then the original names are kept as trivial wrappers:

```python
def _require_batches_impl(packet, *, colorize_fn, suggested_prepare_cmd=None) -> list[dict]:
    # ... actual logic ...

def _require_batches(packet, *, colorize_fn, suggested_prepare_cmd=None) -> list[dict]:
    """Compatibility wrapper for batch presence validation."""
    return _require_batches_impl(packet, colorize_fn=colorize_fn, ...)
```

This pattern is repeated for `_collect_reviewed_files_from_batches`, `_normalize_dimension_list`, `_print_preflight_dimension_scope_notice`, and `_print_import_dimension_coverage_notice`. The wrappers add zero value -- they have identical signatures and just forward. If the intent was to move logic to new files while keeping the old import paths working, the extraction never happened (the `_impl` functions are still in the same file). These should either complete the extraction or be reverted.

**Mid-file bare import:**

Line 157 of `batches.py`:
```python
from .runtime.policy import resolve_batch_run_policy
```

This bare import sits between function definitions, not at the top of the file. It creates a circular-import-avoidance pattern but reads as an afterthought.

**Good: `batch_merge.py` extraction:**

The extraction of merge logic from `batch_core.py` into `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch_merge.py` (206 lines) is genuinely good. However, there's a **semantic change**: the original code used `_accumulate_batch_findings()` which deduped findings into a `dict[str, dict]` by identity key as they were accumulated. The new code uses `_append_batch_findings()` which collects everything into a flat list, then does transitive graph-based merging via `_merge_findings_transitively()`. This is not just an extraction -- it's a behavior change in how duplicate findings from different batches are handled. The new approach is arguably better (it does transitive connected-component merging, which catches more duplicates), but it should be tested carefully as it changes the merge semantics.

**`do_run_batches` refactoring:**

The extraction of closures into module-level functions (`_append_run_log_line`, `_run_batch_task`, `_build_batch_tasks`, `_record_execution_issue`, `_write_run_summary`) using `functools.partial` is a reasonable refactoring. The functions that were closures over `run_log_path`, `run_dir`, etc. are now explicit about their dependencies. However, `_write_run_summary` now takes **21 parameters**, which is excessive:

```python
def _write_run_summary(
    *, run_summary_path, summary_created_at, stamp, runner,
    run_parallel, selected_indexes, successful_batches, failed_batches,
    allow_partial, max_parallel_batches, batch_timeout_seconds,
    batch_max_retries, batch_retry_backoff_seconds, heartbeat_seconds,
    stall_warning_seconds, stall_kill_seconds, immutable_packet_path,
    prompt_packet_path, run_dir, logs_dir, run_log_path,
    batch_status, safe_write_text_fn, colorize_fn, append_run_log_fn,
    interrupted=False, interruption_reason=None,
)
```

A `RunContext` dataclass would be a better interface here.

**Other review/ extractions (15 new files, 4693 lines total):**

The new files (`runner_packets.py`, `runner_process.py`, `runner_failures.py`, `runner_parallel.py`, `import_output.py`, `import_parse.py`, `import_policy.py`, etc.) are well-named and decompose the previously monolithic `runner_helpers.py` and `import_helpers.py`. The naming scheme is clear. `runner_process.py` at 723 lines is the largest, containing `_run_via_popen` and `_run_via_subprocess`. These are large because they manage subprocess lifecycle with stall detection, timeout handling, and live log writing -- the size is justified by the complexity.

**Bug in `external.py`:**

```python
payload = dict(raw_payload)
payload.setdefault("session", session_meta)   # line 418: sets session
payload.pop("session", None)                   # line 419: immediately removes it
```

This is a no-op. The `setdefault` on line 418 is immediately undone by the `pop` on line 419. The intent was probably to use `session_meta` somewhere downstream. As-is, this is dead code that was accidentally introduced. The original code only had `payload.pop("session", None)`.

---

### 3. resolve/ -- Clean Extraction

**Good:**

- `cmd_ignore_pattern` extracted from `cmd.py` to its own `ignore_cmd.py` (82 lines). Clean separation.
- `_save_state_or_exit` and `_save_config_or_exit` extracted to `persist.py` (30 lines). Simple, well-named.
- `render_support.py` extracts three render helpers. However, the new functions take `colorize_fn` and `print_fn` as dependency-injection parameters, even though they're only ever called with the real `colorize` and `print`. This is premature abstraction for testability that adds noise to every call site:

```python
_score_snapshot_or_warn_support(
    state,
    score_snapshot_fn=state_mod.score_snapshot,
    colorize_fn=colorize,
    print_fn=print,
)
```

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/render.py`, three call sites now pass these explicit dependencies where before the functions just used module globals.

- `queue_guard.py` (94 lines) is new functionality: it prevents resolving items that would skip ahead of higher-priority queue items. This is a genuine feature addition, not just extraction.

**New validation -- `validate_note_length`** in `selection.py`:

A minimum 50-character note requirement for `--note` on resolve operations. This is a UX guardrail that prevents empty/trivial notes. Well-implemented.

**Triage guardrail gate** added in `cmd.py`:

```python
if args.status == "fixed":
    require_triage_current_or_exit(
        state=state,
        bypass=bool(getattr(args, "force_resolve", False)),
        attest=getattr(args, "attest", "") or "",
    )
```

This gates the `resolve` command to ensure triage is current before allowing resolution. The `--force-resolve` bypass with attestation is a reasonable escape hatch. However, `require_triage_current_or_exit()` calls `sys.exit(1)`, which means the function name correctly signals the exit-on-failure behavior -- a good practice.

---

### 4. show/ -- Good Extraction

- Functions moved from `cmd.py` to `concerns_view.py` (34 lines) and `dimension_views.py` (149 lines). The extractions are clean -- the same functions, moved to focused files, with proper imports back in `cmd.py`.

- Removal of `check_tool_staleness` call -- this is removed from `show`, `next`, and `status` commands consistently.

- Addition of `no_budget` flag to `resolve_noise()` in `scope.py` with a clean early-return path. No behavior change for existing callers.

- Addition of `print_triage_guardrail_info(state=state)` in `cmd.py` -- consistent with the same addition across all surface commands.

---

### 5. next.py and next_parts/ -- Tier Removal is a Major Semantic Change

**The tier system is being removed.** This is the biggest semantic change in the diff:

- `tier` parameter removed from `_get_items()`, `show_empty_queue()`, `build_work_queue()` calls
- `tier_counts` removed from queue display
- `no_tier_fallback` parameter removed
- Column headers change from `["#", "Tier", ...]` to `["#", "Confidence", ...]`
- Queue items no longer show `T1`/`T2`/`T3`/`T4` labels; they show `confidence` instead
- `_render_tier_navigator()` is deleted entirely
- `_tier_label()` function deleted
- Explain messages change from `"ranked by tier=..., confidence=..."` to `"ranked by confidence=..."`

This is a deliberate architectural shift from the 4-tier priority model to a confidence-based model. The refactoring is thorough -- it touches all the right places. The `render_support.py` extraction cleanly contains the new tier-free rendering logic.

**`QueueBreakdown` dataclass** in `queue_progress.py` is well-designed:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class QueueBreakdown:
    queue_total: int = 0
    plan_ordered: int = 0
    skipped: int = 0
    subjective: int = 0
    workflow: int = 0
    focus_cluster: str | None = None
    focus_cluster_count: int = 0
    focus_cluster_total: int = 0
```

This is a proper domain type that replaces ad-hoc tuple/integer returns. `format_queue_headline()` and `format_queue_block()` provide consistent rendering across all surfaces. This is good engineering -- single source of truth for queue display.

**Defensive `detail` normalization** in `render.py`:

```python
if isinstance(detail, str):
    detail = {"suggestion": detail}
if isinstance(detail, dict):
    detail.setdefault("lines", [])
    detail.setdefault("line", None)
    detail.setdefault("category", None)
    detail.setdefault("importers", None)
    detail.setdefault("count", 0)
```

This is defensive coding that handles unexpected `detail` shapes. It prevents `KeyError`/`TypeError` at render time but also masks data-shape bugs upstream. Debatable, but reasonable for a CLI rendering path.

---

### 6. status_cmd.py and status_parts/render.py -- Heavy Extraction

- `render.py` drops from ~293+ lines to ~48 lines. Three new files absorb the logic:
  - `render_dimensions.py` (149 lines) -- dimension table rendering
  - `render_io.py` (148 lines) -- query writing, ignore summary, tier progress
  - `render_structural.py` (92 lines) -- area workflow rendering

These extractions are clean -- the names describe what they contain, and the imports in `render.py` are straightforward re-imports.

- `objective_backlog` parameter threaded through `show_dimension_table()` and `print_subjective_followup()` to suppress subjective nudges while objective work remains. This is a genuine UX improvement.

---

### 7. helpers/ -- guardrails.py is a Significant Feature Addition

**`guardrails.py` rewrite:**

The module transforms from a simple banner printer (31 lines) to a structured guardrail system (125 lines):

- `TriageGuardrailResult` dataclass for structured detection
- `triage_guardrail_status()` -- pure detection function
- `print_triage_guardrail_info()` -- info-level banner
- `require_triage_current_or_exit()` -- hard gate that `sys.exit(1)`s

The separation of detection from side effects (`triage_guardrail_status` returns a result, `print_triage_guardrail_info` renders it, `require_triage_current_or_exit` enforces it) is good design.

**`queue_progress.py` -- `format_queue_headline` and `format_queue_block`:**

These are well-implemented "single source of truth" formatters. Every surface that shows queue state now goes through the same rendering path. The `format_queue_block` returns `list[tuple[str, str]]` (text, style) pairs rather than printing directly, which is a good separation of concerns.

**`score_update.py` -- `format_delta` inlining:**

The change from `from scan_helpers import format_delta` to a local `_format_delta` is reasonable -- it removes a cross-module dependency for a trivial 4-line function.

---

### 8. scan/ -- Aggressive Extraction of scan_reporting_llm.py

`scan_reporting_llm.py` goes from 367 lines to 11 lines (a re-export shim). All logic moves to `scan/reporting/agent_context.py` (417 lines), `reporting/dimension_table.py` (15 lines), `reporting/integrity_report.py` (288 lines), and `reporting/terminal_output.py` (15 lines).

The tiny 15-line modules (`dimension_table.py`, `terminal_output.py`) seem like premature splitting -- they could remain inline. But the `agent_context.py` extraction is reasonable given its 417-line size.

---

### 9. exclude_cmd.py -- Good Feature Addition

The `exclude` command now cleans up state and plan when a pattern is excluded:

```python
removed_ids = _prune_excluded_findings(state, args.pattern)
if removed_ids:
    state_mod.save_state(state, state_file)
    plan_purged = _purge_removed_ids_from_plan(state_file, removed_ids)
```

This is a genuine improvement -- previously, excluding a path would leave stale findings in state/plan until the next scan. Now it proactively cleans up. Error handling with `try/except` for the plan purge is correct (best-effort).

---

### Summary Verdict

**Good patterns:**
1. `QueueBreakdown` dataclass and centralized formatting (`format_queue_headline`, `format_queue_block`)
2. Audit logging via `append_log_entry()` -- consistent and comprehensive
3. Triage guardrail system with clean separation (detect / render / enforce)
4. `batch_merge.py` extraction with improved transitive merge logic
5. `exclude_cmd.py` now cleans stale state
6. The synthesis-to-triage rename with per-stage IDs is a real improvement
7. All new files import cleanly (verified)

**Problematic patterns:**
1. **`batches.py` compatibility wrappers** are pure noise -- `_impl` suffixed functions with identical-signature wrappers that never completed their extraction
2. **`_write_run_summary` takes 21+ parameters** -- needs a context dataclass
3. **`render_support.py` dependency injection** (`colorize_fn`, `print_fn`) is premature abstraction
4. **`external.py` dead code**: `setdefault` immediately followed by `pop` on same key
5. **`_resolve_target` exported with underscore prefix** -- naming contract violation
6. **`triage_handlers.py` at 1715 lines** is too large, even after extracting into `triage/` subdirectory
7. **`cmd_plan_done()` has grown into a 100+ line orchestrator** that mixes too many concerns

**Risk areas:**
- The `batch_merge.py` change alters finding deduplication semantics (from incremental dict-based to flat-collect-then-graph-merge). This needs test coverage validation.
- The tier removal is pervasive -- any external code or documentation referencing `--tier` or `T1`/`T2` labels will break.
- The triage guardrail in `resolve` calls `sys.exit(1)`, which will block resolution workflows that don't expect it.

```
1→"""Tests for shared queue selection in desloppify.work_queue."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import (
     6→    QueueBuildOptions,
     7→)
     8→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import (
     9→    build_work_queue as _build_work_queue,

    11→
    12→
    13→def build_work_queue(state, **kwargs):
    14→    return _build_work_queue(state, options=QueueBuildOptions(**kwargs))
    15→
    16→
    17→def _finding(
    18→    fid: str,
    19→    *,
    20→    detector: str = "smells",
    21→    file: str = "src/a.py",
    22→    tier: int = 3,
    23→    confidence: str = "medium",
    24→    status: str = "open",
    25→    detail: dict | None = None,
    26→) -> dict:
    27→    return {
    28→        "id": fid,
    29→        "detector": detector,
    30→        "file": file,
    31→        "tier": tier,
    32→        "confidence": confidence,
    33→        "summary": fid,
    34→        "status": status,
    35→        "detail": detail or {},
    36→    }
    37→
    38→
    39→def _state(findings: list[dict], *, dimension_scores: dict | None = None) -> dict:
    40→    return {
    41→        "findings": {f["id"]: f for f in findings},
    42→        "dimension_scores": dimension_scores or {},
    43→    }
    44→
    45→
    46→def test_review_finding_uses_natural_tier():
    47→    review = _finding(
    48→        "review::src/a.py::naming",
    49→        detector="review",
    50→        tier=2,
    51→        detail={"dimension": "naming_quality"},
    52→    )
    53→    mechanical = _finding("smells::src/a.py::x", detector="smells", tier=3)
    54→    state = _state(
    55→        [review, mechanical],
    56→        dimension_scores={
    57→            "Naming quality": {"score": 94.0, "strict": 94.0, "issues": 1}
    58→        },
    59→    )
    60→
    61→    queue = build_work_queue(state, count=None, include_subjective=False)
    62→    by_id = {item["id"]: item for item in queue["items"] if item["kind"] == "finding"}
    63→    assert "review::src/a.py::naming" in by_id
    64→    assert "smells::src/a.py::x" in by_id
    65→

   392→    # threshold=-10 clamps to 0.0 -> score 50 >= 0 -> item excluded
   393→    queue = build_work_queue(
   394→        state, count=None, include_subjective=True, subjective_threshold=-10
   395→    )
   396→    subj_items = [item for item in queue["items"] if item["kind"] == "subjective_dimension"]
   397→    assert subj_items == []
   398→
   399→    # threshold=200 clamps to 100.0 -> score 50 < 100 -> item included
   400→    queue2 = build_work_queue(
   401→        state, count=None, include_subjective=True, subjective_threshold=200
   402→    )
   403→    subj_items2 = [item for item in queue2["items"] if item["kind"] == "subjective_dimension"]
   404→    assert len(subj_items2) >= 1
   405→
   406→
   407→# ── Count limiting ────────────────────────────────────────
   408→
   409→
   410→def test_count_limits_returned_items():
   411→    state = _state(
   412→        [
   413→            _finding("a", tier=2, confidence="high"),
   414→            _finding("b", tier=2, confidence="medium"),
   415→            _finding("c", tier=2, confidence="low"),
   416→        ]
   417→    )
   418→
   419→    queue = build_work_queue(state, count=2, include_subjective=False)
   420→    assert len(queue["items"]) == 2
   421→    assert queue["total"] == 3
   422→
   423→
   424→def test_count_none_returns_all_items():
   425→    state = _state(
   426→        [_finding("a", tier=2), _finding("b", tier=3), _finding("c", tier=4)]
   427→    )
   428→
   429→    queue = build_work_queue(state, count=None, include_subjective=False)
   430→    assert len(queue["items"]) == 3
   431→    assert queue["total"] == 3
   432→
   433→
   434→def test_default_count_is_1():
   435→    state = _state(
   436→        [_finding("a", tier=2), _finding("b", tier=3)]
   437→    )
   438→
   439→    queue = build_work_queue(state, include_subjective=False)
   440→    assert len(queue["items"]) == 1
   441→
   442→
   443→# ── Empty state ───────────────────────────────────────────
   444→
   445→
   446→def test_empty_state_returns_empty_queue():
   447→    queue = build_work_queue({}, count=None, include_subjective=False)
   448→    assert queue["items"] == []
   449→    assert queue["total"] == 0
   450→
   451→
   452→# ── Grouped output ────────────────────────────────────────
   453→
   454→
   455→def test_grouped_output_groups_by_item():
   456→    state = _state(
   457→        [
   458→            _finding("a", file="src/a.py"),
   459→            _finding("b", file="src/b.py"),
   460→        ]
   461→    )
   462→
   463→    queue = build_work_queue(state, count=None, include_subjective=False)
   464→    grouped = queue["grouped"]
   465→    # Default grouping is "item", which groups by file
   466→    assert isinstance(grouped, dict)
   467→
   468→
   469→# ── Status filter ─────────────────────────────────────────
   470→
   471→
   472→def test_status_filter_fixed():
   473→    state = _state(
   474→        [
   475→            _finding("open_one", status="open"),
   476→            _finding("fixed_one", status="fixed"),
   477→        ]
   478→    )
   479→
   480→    queue = build_work_queue(state, status="fixed", count=None, include_subjective=False)
   481→    assert all(item["status"] == "fixed" for item in queue["items"])
   482→    assert len(queue["items"]) == 1
   483→
   484→
   485→def test_status_filter_all():
   486→    state = _state(
   487→        [
   488→            _finding("open_one", status="open"),
   489→            _finding("fixed_one", status="fixed"),
   490→        ]
   491→    )
   492→
   493→    queue = build_work_queue(state, status="all", count=None, include_subjective=False)
   494→    assert len(queue["items"]) == 2
   495→
   496→
   497→# ── Chronic mode ──────────────────────────────────────────
   498→
   499→
   500→def test_chronic_mode_filters_reopened_findings():
   501→    findings = [
   502→        {**_finding("chronic_one"), "reopen_count": 3},
   503→        {**_finding("normal_one"), "reopen_count": 0},
   504→        {**_finding("once_reopened"), "reopen_count": 1},
   505→    ]
   506→    state = _state(findings)
   507→
   508→    queue = build_work_queue(state, chronic=True, count=None, include_subjective=False)
   509→    ids = {item["id"] for item in queue["items"]}
   510→    assert "chronic_one" in ids
   511→    assert "normal_one" not in ids
   512→    assert "once_reopened" not in ids
   513→
   514→
   515→# ── Subjective exclusion from chronic mode ────────────────
   516→
   517→
   518→def test_chronic_mode_excludes_subjective_items():
   519→    state = _state(
   520→        [],
   521→        dimension_scores={
   522→            "Naming quality": {"score": 50.0, "strict": 50.0, "issues": 1},
   523→        },
   524→    )
   525→
   526→    queue = build_work_queue(
   527→        state, chronic=True, count=None, include_subjective=True
   528→    )
   529→    subj_items = [item for item in queue["items"] if item["kind"] == "subjective_dimension"]
   530→    assert subj_items == []
   531→
   532→
   533→# ── Subjective items appear alongside mechanical ──────────
   534→
   535→
   536→def test_subjective_items_always_appear_alongside_mechanical():
   537→    """Subjective items now appear in the queue alongside objective items
   538→    (no backlog gating), sorted by impact."""
   539→    objective_findings = [
   540→        _finding(f"smells::src/{c}.py::x", detector="smells", tier=3)
   541→        for c in "abcd"
   542→    ]
   543→    state = _state(
   544→        objective_findings,
   545→        dimension_scores={
   546→            "Naming quality": {
   547→                "score": 70.0,
   548→                "strict": 70.0,
   549→                "issues": 1,
   550→                "detectors": {
   551→                    "subjective_assessment": {"dimension_key": "naming_quality"},
   552→                },
   553→            },
   554→        },
   555→    )
   556→    state["subjective_assessments"] = {
   557→        "naming_quality": {
   558→            "score": 70.0,
   559→            "needs_review_refresh": True,
   560→            "stale_since": "2026-01-01T00:00:00+00:00",
   561→        }
   562→    }
   563→
   564→    queue = build_work_queue(state, count=None, include_subjective=True)
   565→    ids = [item["id"] for item in queue["items"]]
   566→    subj_ids = [i for i in ids if i.startswith("subjective::")]
   567→
   568→    # Subjective items are no longer suppressed
   569→    assert len(subj_ids) >= 1
   570→    assert len(ids) == 5  # 4 objective + 1 subjective
   571→
   572→
   573→# ── Impact-based ordering ──────────────────────────────────
   574→
   575→
   576→def test_high_impact_subjective_before_low_impact_mechanical():
   577→    """A subjective dimension with high headroom sorts before a mechanical
   578→    finding in a near-perfect dimension."""
   579→    from desloppify.engine._work_queue.ranking import enrich_with_impact, item_sort_key
   580→
   581→    subj_item = {
   582→        "id": "subjective::naming",
   583→        "kind": "subjective_dimension",
   584→        "detail": {"dimension_name": "Naming quality", "strict_score": 43.0},
   585→        "confidence": "medium",
   586→    }
   587→    mech_item = {
   588→        "id": "smells::src/a.py::x",
   589→        "kind": "finding",
   590→        "detector": "smells",
   591→        "confidence": "high",
   592→        "detail": {"count": 1},
   593→    }
   594→
   595→    dimension_scores = {
   596→        "Naming quality": {
   597→            "score": 43.0,
   598→            "strict": 43.0,
   599→            "issues": 3,
   600→            "detectors": {
   601→                "subjective_assessment": {"dimension_key": "naming_quality"},
   602→            },
   603→        },
   604→        "Code quality": {
   605→            "score": 97.0,
   606→            "strict": 97.0,
   607→            "checks": 200,
   608→            "issues": 1,
   609→            "detectors": {
   610→                "smells": {"weighted_failures": 1, "potential": 200},
   611→            },
   612→        },
   613→    }
   614→
   615→    enrich_with_impact([subj_item, mech_item], dimension_scores)
   616→
   617→    # Naming quality has much more headroom (57) than Code quality (3)
   618→    assert subj_item["estimated_impact"] > mech_item["estimated_impact"]
   619→    assert item_sort_key(subj_item) < item_sort_key(mech_item)
   620→
   621→
   622→def test_impact_fallback_when_no_dimension_scores():
   623→    """When no dimension_scores exist, all items get impact 0.0 and
   624→    tiebreakers provide default ordering."""
   625→    from desloppify.engine._work_queue.ranking import enrich_with_impact, item_sort_key
   626→
   627→    items = [
   628→        {
   629→            "id": "smells::src/a.py::x",
   630→            "kind": "finding",
   631→            "detector": "smells",
   632→            "confidence": "high",
   633→            "detail": {"count": 5},
   634→        },
   635→        {
   636→            "id": "smells::src/b.py::y",
   637→            "kind": "finding",
   638→            "detector": "smells",
   639→            "confidence": "medium",
   640→            "detail": {"count": 2},
   641→        },
   642→    ]
   643→
   644→    enrich_with_impact(items, {})
   645→
   646→    assert all(item["estimated_impact"] == 0.0 for item in items)
   647→    # High confidence sorts before medium when impact is equal
   648→    assert item_sort_key(items[0]) < item_sort_key(items[1])
   649→
   650→
   651→def test_collapse_clusters_preserves_order():
   652→    """Cluster meta-item appears at position of first member, not re-sorted."""
   653→    from desloppify.engine._work_queue.plan_order import collapse_clusters
   654→
   655→    plan: dict = {
   656→        "queue_order": [],
   657→        "skipped": {},
   658→        "overrides": {},
   659→        "clusters": {},
   660→        "active_cluster": None,
   661→    }
   662→    plan["clusters"]["auto/unused"] = {
   663→        "name": "auto/unused",
   664→        "auto": True,
   665→        "cluster_key": "auto::unused",
   666→        "finding_ids": ["u1", "u2"],
   667→        "description": "Remove 2 unused findings",
   668→        "action": "desloppify fix unused-imports --dry-run",
   669→        "user_modified": False,
   670→    }
   671→
   672→    # Place a non-cluster item first, then the two cluster members
   673→    items = [
   674→        {"id": "other", "kind": "finding", "detector": "structural",
   675→         "confidence": "medium", "detail": {}},
   676→        {"id": "u1", "kind": "finding", "detector": "unused",
   677→         "confidence": "high", "detail": {}, "estimated_impact": 1.0},
   678→        {"id": "u2", "kind": "finding", "detector": "unused",
   679→         "confidence": "high", "detail": {}, "estimated_impact": 1.0},
   680→    ]
   681→
   682→    result = collapse_clusters(items, plan)
   683→    # Non-cluster item stays at position 0
   684→    assert result[0]["id"] == "other"
   685→    # Cluster meta-item appears at position 1 (where first member was)
   686→    assert result[1]["kind"] == "cluster"
   687→    assert result[1]["id"] == "auto/unused"
   688→    assert len(result) == 2  # other + cluster
   689→
```

```
+# ── QueueBreakdown ───────────────────────────────────────────
+
+
+def test_queue_breakdown_defaults():
+    b = QueueBreakdown()
+    assert b.queue_total == 0
+    assert b.plan_ordered == 0
+    assert b.skipped == 0
+    assert b.subjective == 0
+    assert b.workflow == 0
+    assert b.focus_cluster is None
+    assert b.focus_cluster_count == 0
+    assert b.focus_cluster_total == 0
+
+
+def test_queue_breakdown_frozen():
+    b = QueueBreakdown(queue_total=10, plan_ordered=5)
+    with pytest.raises(AttributeError):
+        b.queue_total = 20  # type: ignore[misc]
+
+
+# ── format_queue_headline ────────────────────────────────────
+
+
+def test_headline_basic():
+    b = QueueBreakdown(queue_total=100)
+    headline = format_queue_headline(b)
+    assert "100 items" in headline
+
+
+def test_headline_with_plan_and_skipped():
+    b = QueueBreakdown(
+        queue_total=1934,
+        plan_ordered=292,
+        skipped=23,
+    )
+    headline = format_queue_headline(b)
+    assert "1934 items" in headline
+    assert "292 planned" in headline
+    assert "23 skipped" in headline
+
+
+def test_headline_omits_zero_segments():
+    b = QueueBreakdown(
+        queue_total=50,
+        plan_ordered=0,
+        skipped=0,
+        subjective=0,
+    )
+    headline = format_queue_headline(b)
+    assert "planned" not in headline
+    assert "skipped" not in headline
+    assert "subjective" not in headline
+
+
+def test_headline_singular_item():
+    b = QueueBreakdown(queue_total=1)
+    headline = format_queue_headline(b)
+    assert "1 item" in headline
+    assert "items" not in headline
+
+
+def test_headline_with_subjective():
+    b = QueueBreakdown(
+        queue_total=50,
+        subjective=5,
+    )
+    headline = format_queue_headline(b)
+    assert "5 subjective" in headline
+
+
+def test_headline_no_plan_mode():
+    """When no plan data, segments are omitted."""
+    b = QueueBreakdown(queue_total=200)
+    headline = format_queue_headline(b)
+    assert "planned" not in headline
+    assert "skipped" not in headline
+
+
+# ── format_queue_block ───────────────────────────────────────
+
+
+def test_block_no_focus_with_plan():
+    b = QueueBreakdown(
+        queue_total=100,
+        plan_ordered=50,
+        skipped=10,
+    )
+    block = format_queue_block(b)
+    texts = [text for text, _style in block]
+    joined = "\n".join(texts)
+    assert "Queue:" in joined
+    assert "desloppify plan queue" in joined
+    assert "Focus:" not in joined
+
+
+def test_block_with_focus():
+    b = QueueBreakdown(
+        queue_total=100,
+        plan_ordered=50,
+        focus_cluster="smart-t1-review",
+        focus_cluster_count=12,
+        focus_cluster_total=50,
+    )
+    block = format_queue_block(b)
+    texts = [text for text, _style in block]
+    styles = [style for _text, style in block]
+    joined = "\n".join(texts)
+    assert "Focus:" in joined
+    assert "smart-t1-review" in joined
+    assert "12/50" in joined
+    assert "Unfocus:" in joined
+    # Focus banner is cyan
+    assert styles[0] == "cyan"
+
+
+def test_block_simple_mode():
+    """No plan — should show 'Start planning' hint."""
+    b = QueueBreakdown(
+        queue_total=200,
+    )
+    block = format_queue_block(b)
+    texts = [text for text, _style in block]
+    joined = "\n".join(texts)
+    assert "Start planning" in joined
+    assert "desloppify plan" in joined
+
+
+def test_block_with_frozen_score():
+    b = QueueBreakdown(
+        queue_total=100,
+        plan_ordered=50,
+    )
+    block = format_queue_block(b, frozen_score=74.4)
+    texts = [text for text, _style in block]
+    joined = "\n".join(texts)
+    assert "74.4" in joined
+    assert "frozen at plan start" in joined
+
+
+# ── plan_aware_queue_breakdown ───────────────────────────────
+
+
+def test_plan_aware_queue_breakdown_basic():
+    mock_result = {
+        "total": 50,
+        "items": [
+            {"kind": "finding"},
+            {"kind": "subjective_dimension"},
+        ],
+    }
+    plan = {
+        "queue_order": ["a", "b", "c"],
+        "skipped": {"c": {"kind": "temporary"}},
+    }
+    with patch(
+        "desloppify.engine.work_queue.build_work_queue",
+        return_value=mock_result,
+    ):
+        breakdown = plan_aware_queue_breakdown({"findings": {}}, plan=plan)
+    assert breakdown.queue_total == 50
+    assert breakdown.plan_ordered == 2  # a, b (c is skipped)
+    assert breakdown.skipped == 1
+    assert breakdown.subjective == 1
+
+
+def test_plan_aware_queue_breakdown_no_plan():
+    mock_result = {
+        "total": 30,
+        "items": [],
+    }
+    with patch(
+        "desloppify.engine.work_queue.build_work_queue",
+        return_value=mock_result,
+    ):
+        breakdown = plan_aware_queue_breakdown({"findings": {}})
+    assert breakdown.queue_total == 30
+    assert breakdown.plan_ordered == 0
+    assert breakdown.skipped == 0
+    assert breakdown.focus_cluster is None
```

```
-def _resolve_python_from_import(
-    module_path: str, import_names: str, source_file: str, scan_root: Path
-) -> list[str]:
-    """Resolve a 'from X import Y' statement to file paths.
-
-    When module_path is dots-only (e.g. 'from . import X, Y'), each imported
-    name might be a submodule, so we resolve each name individually.
-    Otherwise, resolve the module_path as before.
-    """
-    source = (
-        Path(source_file)
-        if Path(source_file).is_absolute()
-        else PROJECT_ROOT / source_file
-    )
-    source_dir = source.parent
-    scan_root_path = Path(scan_root) if not isinstance(scan_root, Path) else scan_root
-
-    # Check if module_path is dots-only (no remainder after the dots)
-    dots_only = all(ch == "." for ch in module_path)
-
-    if dots_only:
-        # from . import X, Y  or  from .. import X, Y
-        # Each imported name could be a submodule in the base directory
-        dots = len(module_path)
-        base = source_dir
-        for _ in range(dots - 1):
-            base = base.parent
-
-        results = []
-        # Parse the import names (handle "X, Y" and "X as Z, Y as W")
-        names = [n.strip().split()[0] for n in import_names.split(",")]
-        for name in names:
-            if not name or name.startswith("(") or name.startswith("#"):
-                continue
-            name = name.strip("()")
-            if not name:
-                continue
-            # Try resolving as a submodule: base/name.py or base/name/__init__.py
-            target = _try_resolve_path(base / name)
-            if target:
-                results.append(target)
-
-        # Also resolve the package __init__.py itself (from . import X can pull from __init__)
-        if not results:
-            target = _try_resolve_path(base)
-            if target:
-                results.append(target)
-        return results
-    else:
-        # Normal case: from .foo import bar, from ..foo.bar import baz
-        results = []
-        target = _resolve_python_import(module_path, source_file, scan_root_path)
-
-        # Also try resolving each imported name as a submodule.
-        # e.g. ``from desloppify.engine._state import filtering`` should
-        # resolve to ``_state/filtering.py``, not just ``_state/__init__.py``.
-        if target and import_names:
-            names = [n.strip().split()[0] for n in import_names.split(",")]
-            for name in names:
-                name = name.strip("()")
-                if not name:
-                    continue
-                submod = _resolve_python_import(
-                    f"{module_path}.{name}", source_file, scan_root_path
-                )
-                if submod:
-                    results.append(submod)
-
-        if target:
-            results.append(target)
-        return results
-
-
-def _resolve_python_import(
-    module_path: str, source_file: str, scan_root: Path
-) -> str | None:
-    """Resolve a Python import to a file path.
-
-    Handles:
-      - Relative imports (starting with .)
-      - Absolute imports within the project
-    """
-    source = (
-        Path(source_file)
-        if Path(source_file).is_absolute()
-        else PROJECT_ROOT / source_file
-    )
-    source_dir = source.parent
-    scan_root_path = Path(scan_root) if not isinstance(scan_root, Path) else scan_root
-
-    if module_path.startswith("."):
-        return _resolve_relative_import(module_path, source_dir)
-    else:
-        return _resolve_absolute_import(module_path, scan_root_path)
-
-
-def _resolve_relative_import(module_path: str, source_dir: Path) -> str | None:
-    """Resolve a relative Python import (from . import X, from .foo import X)."""
-    # Count leading dots
-    dots = 0
-    for ch in module_path:
-        if ch == ".":
-            dots += 1
-        else:
-            break
-
-    remainder = module_path[dots:]
-
-    # Go up (dots - 1) directories from source_dir
-    base = source_dir
-    for _ in range(dots - 1):
-        base = base.parent
-
-    if remainder:
-        parts = remainder.split(".")
-        target_base = base
-        for part in parts:
-            target_base = target_base / part
-    else:
-        target_base = base
-
-    return _try_resolve_path(target_base)
-
-
-def _resolve_absolute_import(module_path: str, scan_root: Path) -> str | None:
-    """Resolve an absolute Python import within the project."""
-    parts = module_path.split(".")
-    # Try from scan root
-    target_base = scan_root.resolve()
-    for part in parts:
-        target_base = target_base / part
-
-    resolved = _try_resolve_path(target_base)
-    if resolved:
-        return resolved
-
-    # Try from project root
-    target_base = PROJECT_ROOT
-    for part in parts:
-        target_base = target_base / part
-
-    return _try_resolve_path(target_base)
-
-
-def find_python_dynamic_imports(path: Path, extensions: list[str]) -> set[str]:
-    """Find module specifiers referenced by importlib.import_module() calls.
-
-    Returns resolved file paths for string-literal arguments to
-    importlib.import_module(), enabling the orphaned detector to
-    recognize dynamically-loaded modules as live.
-    """
-    del extensions  # Python always uses .py
-    targets: set[str] = set()
-    for py_file in path.rglob("*.py"):
-        try:
-            tree = ast.parse(py_file.read_text(), filename=str(py_file))
-        except (SyntaxError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as exc:
-            logger.debug("Skipping unreadable file %s in dynamic import scan: %s", py_file, exc)
-            continue
-        for node in ast.walk(tree):
-            if not isinstance(node, ast.Call):
-                continue
-            func = node.func
-            if (
-                isinstance(func, ast.Attribute)
-                and func.attr == "import_module"
-                and isinstance(func.value, ast.Name)
-                and func.value.id == "importlib"
-                and node.args
-                and isinstance(node.args[0], ast.Constant)
-                and isinstance(node.args[0].value, str)
-            ):
-                spec = node.args[0].value
-                # Resolve the module specifier to a file path
-                resolved = _resolve_absolute_import(spec, path)
-                if resolved:
-                    targets.add(resolved)
-                else:
-                    # Fall back to the raw specifier so _is_dynamically_imported
-                    # can still do substring matching
-                    targets.add(spec)
-    return targets
-
-
-def _try_resolve_path(target_base: Path) -> str | None:
-    """Try to resolve a module base path to an actual file."""
-    # foo.py
-    candidate = Path(str(target_base) + ".py")
-    if candidate.is_file():
-        return str(candidate.resolve())
-
-    # foo/__init__.py
-    candidate = target_base / "__init__.py"
-    if candidate.is_file():
-        return str(candidate.resolve())
-
-    # foo/ (directory with __init__.py)
-    if target_base.is_dir():
-        init = target_base / "__init__.py"
-        if init.is_file():
-            return str(init.resolve())
-
-    return None
+__all__ = ["build_dep_graph", "find_python_dynamic_imports"]
```
